# Sources

Per-source-document inventory. Each row points to a `<source-slug>.md` file that lists the section files derived from that source, with provenance metadata (commit, date, authors, ingestion date).

The pilot batch (2026-05-13) covers three endo top-level / docs-level files. The rest of the endo corpus (~70 substantive markdown files: top-level, `docs/`, per-package READMEs, per-package `docs/` and `doc/` directories) is queued for future ingestion batches; the index will grow as those are filed.

## External code-comment fragments

Longform comments distilled from upstream source files. Each row captures the `source_kind: comment-fragment` schema introduced 2026-05-15 by `entries/2026/05/15/205458Z-message-liaison-0460cf.md`: a specific line range in a specific file, anchored to a path-specific commit sha for idempotency, with a `comment_subject` that names the cohesive argument cluster the comment makes. Sections sit under the source file the same way they do for repo and paper sources.

TODO share more code with that file and keycollection-operators.js*; (b) above `bagIterIsSuperbag` — *We should be able to use this for iterIsSuperset as well. The code is identical*. **Single most structurally interesting move**: *the same merge-iterator + adapter pyramids support two different algebras*. Sets form a Boolean lattice (presence-only); bags form a multiplicity lattice (counts accumulate / min / subtract). Bag union *sums counts* (`xc + yc`); bag intersection takes *min* (`min(xc, yc)`); bag superset compares *counts* (`xc >= yc`); the set algebra is *bag-algebra-with-counts-clipped-to-{0n,1n}*. Three structural layers: (1) `bagWindowResort` is the entry-variant (`[T, bigint][]` vs cycle 123's `T[]`); uses `assertNoDuplicateKeys` (vs set's `assertNoDuplicates`); same §raw-JS-iterator-doesn't-harden escape hatch with *unfrozen-value-does-not-escape-this-file* invariant; tuple-shaped `[null, 0n]` terminating value (vs cycle 123's bare `null`); (2) `merge` produces `Iterable<[T, xCount, yCount]>` with *six let-buffer variables* (cycle 123 used four; bags need `xc`/`yc` for actual multiplicities); (3) five bagIterOps + two adapter pyramids producing **5 exports** (vs cycle 123's 6) — no `bagDisjointUnion` because bag union *already sums counts*, no `bagIterCompare` because bag comparison lives in cycle 104's compareKeys via cycle 120's `makeCompareCollection`. `bagify` adapter re-tags via `makeBagOfEntries(...)` preserving cycle 115's *canonical copyBag internal form* invariant (every count >= 1n; absent keys mean count-zero); the §`if (mc >= 1n) push` filter in `bagIterDisjointSubtract` preserves the invariant on output. The factory `makeMessageBreakpointTester(optionName)` reads an env-option-named JSON record and produces a tester with `getBreakpoints` / `setBreakpoints` / `shouldBreakpoint`. §Three-axis match grammar: MatchStringTag (recipient class) × MatchMethodName × MatchCountdown — each axis admits `'*'` wildcard; countdown `'*'` always breakpoint, `0` always, positive integer decrements until zero. **Single most structurally interesting move**: the §external↔internal transpose — external JSON is `{tag: {method: countdown}}` (human-organized), internal table is `{method: {tag: countdown}}` (lookup-organized because shouldBreakpoint knows methodName before recipient); setBreakpoints transposes the index for fast lookup. §simplifyTag strips `'Alleged: '` or `'DebugName: '` prefix (one level only); setBreakpoints validation *enforces canonical simple tags* — *Just use simple tag X rather than Y* (pushes prefix-stripping to configuration time, not per-call match time). §shouldBreakpoint flow: method-or-wildcard fallback + tag-or-wildcard fallback + `'*'`-or-`0`-always-breakpoint shortcut + §in-place-decrement on internal table. §getBreakpoints-returns-original-not-mutated invariant: external `breakpoints` variable is NOT mutated; only the internal table's countdowns decrement. §default-argument-to-stored-breakpoints idiom (`setBreakpoints()` with no arg) doubles as a *reset-countdowns* call. §env-option-yields-undefined-when-unset discipline: factory returns `undefined` rather than a no-op tester — caller checks `if (tester)` and skips entirely (the §zero-cost-when-unset property). §`__proto__: null` on internal records prevents prototype-pollution lookup; §freeze on exports prevents method replacement; no `harden` (predates the @endo/harden migration). §The async-call-debugging-pain-point: eventual-send delivers later than the call site; this file lets the user say *break on the third call to `.send` on any object tagged `'wallet'`* via JSON env var. Caller's `Promise.resolve(value).then` resolves immediately. §primitive-bypass-via-fake-record. §TODO at top: *Consolidate with `isPrimitive` that's currently in `@endo/pass-style`. | [symbol.js Hilbert-Hotel encoding for passable symbols](endo--packages-pass-style-src-symbol-js.md) | endojs/endo | packages/pass-style/src/symbol.js | 1-123 | The *passable-symbol surface* for @endo/pass-style. Defines what counts as a *passable symbol* (predicate + assertion), the bidirectional encoding between symbols and their wire-form string names, and an escape hatch for non-passable symbols. **Single most structurally interesting move**: the §Hilbert-Hotel encoding, *named explicitly in the file's JSDoc as a load-bearing trick*. The naive encoding `name → Symbol.for(name)` claims *every* string for registered symbols, leaving *no* room for well-known symbols. Hilbert-Hotel encoding shifts: `"@@iterator"` → `Symbol.iterator` (well-known); `"@@@@iterator"` → `Symbol.for("@@iterator")` (registered, shifted by one more `@@`); plain `"foo"` → `Symbol.for("foo")`. Two encoding spaces are disjoint because well-known names don't start with `@@` — asserted at module load as a host-platform invariant (§fail-at-load-not-at-use discipline). §Two-kinds-of-passable-symbols: registered (via `Symbol.for/keyFor`) + well-known (static symbol values on `Symbol` constructor); anonymous `Symbol(description)` excluded. §`wellKnownSymbolNames` Map built at module load via `ownKeys(Symbol)` + filter + identity-keyed-Map<symbol-value, `@@name`>. §Three-case-decoder `nameForPassableSymbol`: returns undefined for non-passable; double-prefix for registered-with-`@@`-prefix; `@@`-prefix for well-known; plain for registered. §Three-case-parser `passableSymbolForName` mirrors the encoder with the §forward-compatibility-via-throw discipline — *if name begins with `@@` it may encode a registered symbol from a future version of JavaScript, but it is not one we can decode yet, so throw*; §throw-rather-than-lose-identity rather than silently falling through to `Symbol.for` (the receiver might *later* gain the well-known symbol; silent fall-through would map pre-upgrade and post-upgrade messages to different symbols, breaking equality). §`AtAtPrefixPattern = /^@@(.*)$/` compiled and hardened at module load. §`unpassableSymbolForName(name) = Symbol(name)` — one-line escape hatch. §hideAndHardenFunction asymmetry: only on `assertPassableSymbol`. Foundational across the @endo/pass-style stack: cycle 71's passStyleOf dispatches `typeof === 'symbol'` here; cycle 69's encodeToSmallcaps and cycle 81's encodePassable both use `nameForPassableSymbol`; cycle 134's remotable.js's `@@toStringTag` allowance depends on this file's encoding. §Symbol-passability-as-pass-style-leaf observation. the result is a binary that is mode-selectable at startup but state-light at runtime: no PID files, no lock files, no internal singleton dance, no per-platform service-manager glue inside the daemon*; Parent: [endoclaw](endoclaw.md). **Single most structurally interesting move**: §a-design-pattern-not-a-new-capability — *A design pattern — not a new capability — for agents that initiate conversations with the host on a schedule. The agent composes Timer (for scheduling) with existing messaging (`send`) and granted data capabilities (email, calendar, filesystem) to produce periodic briefings, reminders, and alerts*; §three-named-categories-of-Endo-feature-classification: §capability (grants new authority) + §UI-feature (does not grant new authority — cycle 255) + §design-pattern (composes existing capabilities without introducing new ones — cycle 257); Parent: [endoclaw](endoclaw.md). **Single most structurally interesting move**: §voice-input-is-a-UI-concern-not-a-capability-concern — *This is a UI feature, not a capability — it does not grant the agent any new authority*; §the-agent-cannot-distinguish-voice-input-from-typed-input (capability-by-invariance); §the-transcribed-text-enters-the-system-as-a-normal-message + §the-agent-just-sees-text; §when-a-new-input-modality-can-be-projected-to-an-existing-substrate-(text), §project-it-and-don't-give-the-agent-a-new-input-API; Cons: requires internet + Chrome sends audio to Google + limited language support + no offline) + Option B Local Whisper (Pros: fully offline + privacy-preserving + better accuracy; Cons: native binary or WASM + ~75MB model + higher CPU) + Option C Daemon-side transcription (Pros: offloads compute from UI + works for remote/Docker; Cons: latency + requires daemon to bundle Whisper); §distinct-from-cycle-250's-Options-Considered-with-preferred (where one option was named preferred); §when-the-design-doesn't-prefer-one-option, §enumerate-them + §give-each-Pros-Cons + §the-implementer-chooses-based-on-deployment-context; | [blog.modelcontextprotocol.io — The 2026-07-28 MCP Specification Release Candidate (PAPERS-LANE OUT-OF-BAND; FIRST AFTER 144+ BLOCKED)](mcp-blog--2026-07-28-release-candidate.md) | blog.modelcontextprotocol.io | posts/2026-07-28-release-candidate/ | blog post | **§Five-named-deliverables-of-a-release + §the-headline-change-IS-statelessness + §multiple-SEPs-collectively-deliver-an-architectural-change (six SEPs for statelessness alone; twenty SEPs total referenced) + §Before-and-after-code-block-pair-with-named-version-numbers + §the-practical-effect-on-production-IS-the-value-statement (three named things removed + three named replacements) + §stateless-protocol-stateful-applications named distinction + §the-explicit-handle-pattern-makes-state-visible-to-the-model-not-hidden-in-transport-metadata + §opaque-server-state-echoed-by-client-as-resumption-mechanism (requestState) + §expose-the-routing-discriminator-as-an-HTTP-header-not-in-the-body (Mcp-Method + Mcp-Name) + §cache-control-shape-as-replacement-for-SSE-polling (ttlMs + cacheScope) + §formalize-existing-key-names-in-spec (W3C Trace Context) + §reverse-DNS-IDs-as-named-identifier-convention-for-extensions + §server-rendered-UI-with-three-named-defenses (sandbox + pre-declaration + uniform-back-channel) + §feature-graduates-to-an-extension-as-named-demotion-from-core (Tasks demoted) + §per-deprecated-feature-named-replacement + §twelve-month-minimum-between-deprecation-and-removal as named lifecycle policy + §named-security-constraints-when-adopting-more-expressive-schema-language (JSON Schema 2020-12) + §named-breaking-change-with-named-affected-consumer-pattern (-32002 → -32602) + §the-breaking-change-IS-the-foundation-for-non-breaking-future-changes + §conformance-suite-as-gating-mechanism-for-Final-status + §SEP-numbering-as-traceable-history-of-protocol-decisions + §two-co-maintainers-named-with-named-role + §the-future-direction-named-in-a-current-release**. Status **Release Candidate** (RC locked 2026-05-21; final ships 2026-07-28; ten-week validation window). **PAPERS-LANE OUT-OF-BAND INGEST AT MAINTAINER REQUEST — first papers-lane source after 144+ blocked consecutive cycles.** **Single most structurally interesting move**: §the-headline-change-IS-statelessness — *MCP is now stateless at the protocol layer; six SEPs work together to get there (SEP-2575 handshake-removed + SEP-2567 session-removed + SEP-2260 server-initiated-requests-only-while-processing-client-request + SEP-2322 Multi-Round-Trip-Requests + SEP-2243 Mcp-Method-and-Mcp-Name-headers + SEP-2549 ttlMs-and-cacheScope)*; §when-a-protocol-undergoes-a-major-architectural-change, §enumerate-the-Specification-Enhancement-Proposals-that-collectively-deliver-it + §each-SEP-is-a-self-contained-change-but-they-work-together; §first-explicit-observation in library of §multiple-SEPs-collectively-deliver-an-architectural-change as named-governance-shape. §The-practical-effect-on-production-IS-the-value-statement: *A remote MCP server that previously needed sticky sessions, a shared session store, and deep packet inspection at the gateway can now run behind a plain round-robin load balancer, route traffic on an Mcp-Method header, and let clients cache tools/list responses for as long as the server's ttlMs permits*; §three-named-things-removed + §three-named-replacements; §when-a-protocol-change-affects-operations-not-just-API, §name-the-infrastructure-changes-explicitly + §the-replacement-shape-IS-the-evidence-the-change-improves-operations. §Stateless-protocol-stateful-applications named distinction — §removing-the-protocol-level-session-does-not-mean-your-application-has-to-be-stateless; §the-explicit-handle-pattern (mint a basket_id from a tool + model passes it back as ordinary argument); §the-explicit-handle-makes-state-visible-to-the-model-not-hidden-in-transport-metadata; §the-model-can-compose-handles-across-tools-reason-about-them-and-hand-them-off; §sibling-pattern-to-cycle-244's-the-formula-IS-the-stable-handle. §Multi-Round-Trip-Requests (SEP-2322) — InputRequiredResult with opaque requestState echoed by client on retry; §any-server-instance-can-pick-that-retry-up-because-everything-it-needs-is-in-the-payload; §when-an-async-protocol-must-become-sync-or-stateless, §encode-the-server's-resumption-state-as-an-opaque-payload-the-client-echoes-back; §sibling-pattern-to-cycle-249's-out-of-band-communications (three-cycles-with-deferred-response-mechanisms: 241 + 249 + 251). §Mcp-Method-and-Mcp-Name headers (SEP-2243) — routing-without-deep-packet-inspection + §servers-reject-requests-where-the-headers-and-body-disagree; §the-headers-IS-the-routing-discriminator-extracted-from-the-body; §first-explicit-observation in library of §expose-the-routing-discriminator-as-an-HTTP-header-not-in-the-body. §ttlMs-and-cacheScope (SEP-2549) — modeled on HTTP Cache-Control; §the-cache-control-shape-IS-the-replacement-for-SSE-polling; §two-named-cache-fields (ttlMs + cacheScope for per-user vs shared). §W3C-Trace-Context-propagation (SEP-414) — locking-down-traceparent-tracestate-and-baggage-key-names-in-_meta + §formalize-existing-SDK-practice; §the-trace-can-follow-a-tool-call-through-the-client-SDK-the-MCP-server-and-downstream-and-show-up-as-a-single-span-tree. §Extensions-as-first-class (SEP-2133) — reverse-DNS-IDs (e.g., `io.modelcontextprotocol/clientInfo`) + Extensions-Track-in-SEP-process + ext-*-repositories with delegated maintainers + version-independently-of-the-specification; §sibling-pattern-to-cycle-248's-custom-MIME-type (two-cycles-with-named-extension-identifier-conventions). §MCP-Apps (SEP-1865) — server-rendered-HTML-in-sandboxed-iframe + §three-named-defenses (sandbox + pre-declaration ahead of time for prefetch/cache/security-review + UI talks back via same JSON-RPC base protocol so every UI-initiated action goes through same audit/consent path as direct tool call); §sibling-pattern-to-cycle-238's-three-named-SSRF-vectors-and-three-named-defenses. §Tasks-graduates-to-an-extension — §named-demotion-from-core (was experimental in 2025-11-25); §production-use-surfaced-enough-redesign-that-the-right-home-for-it-is-an-extension; §task-creation-is-server-directed; §tasks/list-removed-because-it-can't-be-scoped-safely-without-sessions; §first-explicit-observation in library of §feature-graduates-to-an-extension-as-named-demotion-from-core. §Authorization-hardening — six SEPs (2468 iss validation per RFC 9207 + 837 OpenID Connect application_type during DCR + 2352 credential binding to issuer + 2207 refresh tokens + 2350 scope accumulation + 2351 .well-known discovery suffix) for OAuth/OpenID Connect alignment; §the-future-direction-named: *In a future version, clients will be expected to reject responses that omit iss, so authorization servers should begin supplying it now*; §first-explicit-observation in library of §the-future-direction-named-in-a-current-release as named forward-compatibility discipline. §Three-core-features-deprecated (Roots + Sampling + Logging) under new feature lifecycle policy (SEP-2577) with §per-deprecated-feature-named-replacement (Roots → tool parameters/resource URIs/server config; Sampling → direct LLM provider API integration; Logging → stderr for stdio + OpenTelemetry for structured); §annotation-only-deprecations + §methods-continue-to-work-in-this-release-and-every-version-within-a-year + §removing-any-of-them-requires-a-separate-SEP; §coalescing-IS-the-back-pressure-mechanism-on-restart; §when-a-buffer-of-missed-events-accumulates-during-downtime, §coalesce-them-into-one-event-with-a-named-count + §the-count-IS-the-signal-the-consumer-needs-not-the-individual-events. §Tick-events-as-messages-not-iterator-values (Design-Decision-1) — §three-named-benefits (persistence + ordering + replay); §backoff-caps-at-tick-deadline — §the-cap-IS-the-anti-livelock-mechanism. §thisDiesIfThatDies + onCancel two named lifetime mechanisms: §context.onCancel-for-cleanup-on-cancellation + §context.thisDiesIfThatDies(agentId)-for-lifetime-linkage; the Uint8Array stores bytes [1, 0], and when read as Uint16: little-endian platforms give 1 (LSB first: 0x0001), big-endian platforms give 256 (MSB first: 0x0100); the discrepancy IS the evidence*; §when-the-host-platform's-byte-order-must-be-known-at-runtime, §write-known-bytes-via-one-view-and-read-them-via-another-view + §the-discrepancy-IS-the-evidence; §no-NodeJS-API-call-required + §no-`os.endianness()`-import + §the-detection-is-pure-JavaScript-language-feature-only; §sibling-pattern-to-cycle-241's-resolve-callback-captured-via-closure (the-canonical-tricks-are-load-bearing). §The-test-buffer-uses-two-bytes-not-four — §two-bytes-is-sufficient-because-Uint16-reads-two-bytes; §when-detecting-byte-order, §use-the-smallest-unit-that-distinguishes-the-orderings; §the-bytes-[1, 0]-are-not-arbitrary: §the-1-is-the-distinguishing-bit + §the-0-is-the-don't-care-padding + §the-test-distinguishes-1-from-256-because-256-is-1-shifted-by-8; §when-the-test-relies-on-a-single-distinguishing-bit, §put-the-bit-in-the-byte-that-the-target-endianness-treats-as-the-LSB. §Module-load-evaluation-memoizes-the-result: `export const hostIsLittleEndian = isHostLittleEndian();` runs at module load + the result is frozen into a module-level constant; §the-host's-byte-order-doesn't-change-between-loads-of-the-same-module + §so-the-one-time-evaluation-IS-the-correct-shape; §when-a-fact-about-the-host-platform-is-stable-for-the-lifetime-of-the-module, §evaluate-it-at-module-load-and-export-it-as-a-constant-not-as-a-function; §sibling-pattern-to-cycle-239's-GET_INTERFACE_GUARD-named-constant. §The-name-shifts-from-predicate-to-state: §the-helper-function-is-named-`isHostLittleEndian` (predicate shape; verb-first asks the question) + §the-exported-constant-is-named-`hostIsLittleEndian` (state shape; subject-first IS the answer); §the-name-IS-the-shape + §when-a-function-returns-a-fact-and-the-fact-is-cached-as-a-constant, §rename-from-predicate-to-state-form; §first-explicit-observation in library of §the-name-shifts-from-predicate-to-state-when-the-function-result-is-cached. §The-named-form-over-the-IIFE-form: the canonical IIFE shape `(() => { ... })()` would be terser, but the author chose to name the function `isHostLittleEndian` separately + call it on the next line; §the-named-form-makes-the-function-debuggable + §makes-the-stack-trace-meaningful-if-the-function-throws + §makes-the-function-testable-in-isolation; §when-an-IIFE-would-suffice-but-the-function's-purpose-is-named-able, §prefer-the-named-form-over-the-anonymous-IIFE + §the-name-IS-the-documentation; §sibling-to-cycle-241's-`function postpone(...)`-debug-name (two-cycles-with-names-given-to-functions-that-didn't-need-them-syntactically). §Why-endianness-matters-at-this-layer: §the-lp32-package (length-prefix-32) reads and writes streams framed by 32-bit length prefixes; §the-cache-key-is-the-source-not-the-(source,target)-pair; §recognize-when-the-algorithm's-natural-product-is-larger-than-the-API's-natural-product + §cache-the-larger-product; §sibling to cycle 221 @endo/bundle-source's SHA-512-content-addressed-source-map-cache. §Honest-attribution to third-party (datavis-tech/graph-data-structure MIT-licensed adaptation by Curran Kelleher); §when-a-getter-exposes-internal-state, §return-a-fresh-copy-to-prevent-mutation. §Chainable-API via return this for mutating methods (addNode + addEdge + removeNode + removeEdge); §the-chainable-style-IS-the-domain-specific-language for building graphs. §The-relax-step with classical-algorithm-name; Status **Not Started** (Parent: endoclaw). **Single most structurally interesting move**: §the-agent-never-sees-the-token — *The OAuth interface has no method that returns the credential. The agent can use the service but cannot extract the token to forward it elsewhere or use it on a different endpoint. This is the canonical ocap pattern: authority to use, not authority to delegate outside the capability graph*; §a-structural-invariant-of-the-interface-not-a-runtime-check; §when-a-capability-must-not-leak-its-underlying-credential, §design-the-interface-so-no-method-returns-the-credential + §the-credential-only-flows-through-the-call-not-through-a-getter; §sibling to cycle 224 daemon-web-gateway's bearer-token-as-formula-ID — §two-different-capability-shapes-for-credential-handling (224 makes formula-ID-IS-the-token; 234 hides the token entirely). §Two-facet-control-pair (OAuth + OAuthControl) consistent with cycle 226's canonical template + §four-named-control-methods (setScopes + setAllowedPaths + setReadOnly + refresh) + revoke + help; §the-control-facet-has-more-methods-than-the-capability-facet. §Path-restrictions-structural-confinement (*An agent with Gmail read access cannot call the Calendar API on the same Google domain*); §two-layer-confinement (baseUrl scope + path allowlist within scope); §subdomain-vs-path-distinction (the domain is not the confinement boundary; the path is); §sibling to cycle 226 endoclaw-network-fetch's origin-allowlist-is-structural. §Read-only-mode (setReadOnly toggle restricts to GET/HEAD); §sibling to cycle 226 endoclaw-browser's setReadOnly (two-cycles-with-the-setReadOnly-mode-toggle). §Six-step-OAuth-flow with §step-5-as-five-substep-internal-flow (prepend baseUrl + validate path + check method + inject Authorization header + make request). §Token-refresh-handled-transparently + §explicit-control-via-control-facet-method (refresh); §lifecycle-events-handled-transparently-by-default. §Caretaker-revocation with §two-layered-revocation (local authoritative invalidate-the-exo + optionally-revoke-the-OAuth-token-with-the-provider remote best-effort); §the-optionally-acknowledges that provider-side-revocation might fail. §Built-on-endoclaw-network-fetch — §a-higher-level-capability-is-a-wrapper-around-a-lower-level-capability; §cycle-226's-network-fetch is the substrate; §cycle-234's-OAuth is the §authenticated-decorator on top. §Use-Cases-section enumerates five named OAuth targets (Gmail + Google Calendar + Notion + Todoist + Any OAuth2-compatible API) with §enumerate-concrete-use-cases-and-then-generalize discipline. §Twenty-seventh-honest-design-evolution-record family member; §when-you-substitute-tokens-in-source-text, §size-the-replacement-to-match-the-original-length-so-source-maps-stay-aligned; §the-cost-of-deviation-is-broken-source-maps + §the-cost-of-conformance-is-padding-the-substitute; Status **Partially implemented** (Familiar-side Ready in five named modules; Chat-side and Layer-6 Not Yet). **Single most structurally interesting move**: §the-six-layer-defense-in-depth — each layer numbered + named + paired with the attack-class it blocks: Layer 1 CSP (CSP-governed network requests) + Layer 2 Electron request interception (CSP bypasses: service worker fetch, navigation, future features) + Layer 3 DNS poisoning (DNS prefetch, speculative connects) + Layer 4 navigation delegate (window navigation to external URLs) + Layer 5 WebRTC disabled (ICE-candidate exfiltration via STUN/TURN) + Layer 6 iframe sandbox (iframe escape attempts: top-navigation, popups); §each-layer-named-and-numbered-with-its-confinement-purpose; §defense-in-depth-with-named-attack-per-layer — §a-guest-would-need-to-bypass-all-of-them-simultaneously; §the-multi-layer-synthesis (cycle 220 names the stack; Type 2 = forum; Type 3 = outliner). §Seven-built-in-reply-types (Reply / Edit / Deletion / Move / Pro / Con / Supporting-Evidence) each with named-renderer-interpretation. §User-defined-reply-types-as-string-tags: "A channel administrator can add new reply type strings at runtime [...] reply type is just a string tag on a reply message — no protocol changes needed". §Last-write-wins-with-edit-queue: no locking, no OT, no CRDT; the edit queue is the history; earlier edits remain reviewable. §Private-reply-trees-as-capability-grant: §maps-directly-to-Endo's-capability-model. §Layered-confidentiality: private subtrees within private subtrees with narrower participant sets. §Edit-queue with deletion-of-deletions: recursive deletion targeting; deletions can target deletions, restoring the edit they targeted. §Avatar-lineage as overlapping circles ordered left-to-right by edit sequence (z-index-as-edit-order encoding). §Global-Playback chronological walk-through with §playback-with-viewer-history-aware-display (seen-before nodes get diff-markup; first-time nodes get current-content-only). §Viewer-side-administration with retroactive-blocking: blocking slices out user's mutations from viewer's entire history; §block-propagates-to-future-invitees; creates §personalized-unique-view per viewer. §Custom-attenuation-code in SES-Compartment: §even-untrusted-attenuator-code-can-at-worst-interrupt-cannot-escalate. §Reference-scoping-no-upward-traversal — §the-file-system-principle: "endowing a directory does not endow upward"; per-item watch/unwatch from the components; automatic-GC-on-unmount via CapTP reference drop. §Confirm-on-cancel-two-click — single click enters confirm state (grows + red border + "Click again to cancel" tooltip); second click within 3 seconds executes; click elsewhere or timeout reverts. §Deletion-and-cancellation distinction: × removes pet name (naming operation; "I no longer want this name"); indicator cancels incarnation (lifecycle operation; "stop this thing"); cancellation may occur as deletion side-effect via GC if formula has no remaining pins/names. §Pinned-capabilities-survive-pet-name-deletion via PINS directory — §pin-as-GC-anchor; sibling to cycle 175 harden-selector's §pin-on-first-install. §Disabled-for-special-names (AGENT/SELF/HOST uppercase) — button renders indicator but no click handler; §disabled-but-still-shown for §uncancellable-substrate-capabilities. §ASCII-visual-layout-diagram of inventory rows with three state examples (filled / pulsing / hollow); sibling to cycle 200 worker-rust-xs's §ASCII-architecture-diagram. §InterfaceGuard-with-five-methods (sibling to cycles 134/136/142/148/150 pass-style-cluster). §Five-Design-Decisions canonical format. §Additive-API discipline; old clients unaffected; existing `cancel` CLI command exercises the underlying mechanism. §Upgrade-Considerations-section with two named concerns (crash-recovery-of-status-state + pre-upgrade-worker-status-reporting). §Design-consolidation-in-Prompt-section — `live-reference-indicator` prior design folded in (proposed similar status dots with cancel popover); §thirteenth-honest-design-evolution-record family member with §a-new-shape (consolidation rather than removal/revision/discard). §Tier-1 borrowing: §one-button-two-functions + §five-state-color-coding + §confirm-on-cancel-via-two-click-3-second-timeout + §deletion-and-cancellation-distinct + §pin-as-GC-anchor + §disabled-but-still-shown + §coalesced-watcher-protocol + §three-method-API (watch+unwatch+watchAll) + §E.sendOnly-fire-and-forget + §watch(id)-immediately-publishes-current-status + §watcher-scoped-to-own-pet-store + §single-watcher-per-component-tree + §ASCII-art-as-design-prose + §five-Design-Decisions canonical format + §additive-API + §reuse-existing-mechanism + §design-consolidation-recorded-in-Prompt-section + §Upgrade-Considerations-section. §Synthesis-target: slot machine library §casino-operator-dashboard — §one-button-two-functions for §game-state-indicator-and-stop-button; §five-state-color-coding for §game-state-display; §confirm-on-cancel-two-click for §stopping-running-game; §coalesced-watcher-protocol for §dashboard-with-N-games (single watcher streams all transitions, client mutates watched set as games scroll in/out of view); §E.sendOnly for §UI-state-updates-that-don't-need-acknowledgment. §three-different-ways-to-emulate-private-state in JavaScript across @endo: §pumpkin-sentinel (cycle 199 memoize) + §registered-symbol (cycle 197 panic) + §WeakMap (this cycle); §the-WeakMap-approach-is-the-only-one-that-also-serves-as-brand-check by-construction. §Method-binding-pre-defineProperty on the WeakMap (`buffers.has = buffers.has` etc) to avoid post-hoc prototype lookups. §Five-throw-methods on intermediate prototype (resize / transfer / transferToFixedLength / transferToImmutable; slice stays mutable; sliceToImmutable returns new immutable) + §six-getter-overrides (byteLength / detached false / maxByteLength / resizable false / immutable true / toStringTag). §Brand-check-via-`getBuffer(this)` on every accessor — even getters that don't need the buffer-value call it for the side-effect (throw TypeError on non-emulated instances). §Three-tier-fallback: `ArrayBuffer.prototype.transfer` (Node 21+) → `structuredClone({transfer: [...]})` (Node 17+) → undefined (graceful degradation; ponyfill+shim fail to initialize on known-deficient platforms). §Zero-length-slice-as-genuine-ArrayBuffer-enforcement — `arrayBufferSlice(arrayBuffer, 0, 0)` before structuredClone (the side-effect is enforcing arrayBuffer is a genuine exotic object). §Capture-before-scuttled pre-lockdown discipline — eight intrinsics captured at module load with the named comment "Capture structuredClone before it can be scuttled" (sibling to cycle 181 base64's Reflect.apply capture, cycle 199 trampoline's classic-uncurry-this, cycle 175 harden-selector's pin-on-first-install). §Belt-and-suspenders-freeze on must-not-escape factory `makeImmutableArrayBufferInternal` — honor-system-discipline-with-defense-in-depth. §Modern-shim-practice-frowns-on-conditional-installation — named-shim-philosophy with rationale (changes to proposal don't break old code depending on old shim); §warning-not-error-when-overwriting with §TODO-comment naming the known-broken-interaction with lockdown's frozen primordials. §Encapsulated-genuine-ArrayBuffer-with-exclusive-access — security demands exclusive access; immutability enforced by never modifying it. §Two-named-motivations: (1) §ROM-vs-RAM Moddable XS rationale — embedded systems need to place voluminous fixed data into ROM; (2) §By-copy network protocol rationale — the network capability protocol Endo speaks treats byte-arrays as bulk data transmitted by copy; JavaScript strings reflect by-copy via immutability; immutable ArrayBuffer is the symmetric shape for byte-arrays; `@endo/pass-style` + `@endo/marshal` would consume this. §Nineteenth-member of §small-files-with-large-knowledge-density family. §Tier-1 borrowing: §Ponyfill+Shim pattern (full version) + §Purposeful-Violation-section + §concordance-sniff-defense via Symbol.toStringTag + §six+-named-Caveats + §WeakMap-as-emulated-private-field-AND-brand-check + §method-binding-pre-defineProperty + §intermediate-prototype-inheriting-from-the-host-prototype + §five-throw-methods-with-two-distinct-error-messages + §slice-stays-mutable-vs-sliceToImmutable + §brand-check-via-getBuffer-on-every-accessor + §three-tier-fallback-with-named-known-deficient-platforms + §zero-length-slice-as-genuine-ArrayBuffer-enforcement + §capture-before-scuttled + §belt-and-suspenders-freeze + §modern-shim-practice-frowns-on-conditional-installation + §shim-still-runs-after-native-implementation + §warning-not-error-when-overwriting + §two-named-motivations-from-orthogonal-domains + §plain-JavaScript-not-Hardened-JavaScript-disclaimer + §encapsulated-genuine-ArrayBuffer-with-exclusive-access. §Synthesis-target: slot machine library §immutable-game-state-shapes can §borrow-the-WeakMap-as-emulated-private-field-AND-brand-check directly; Status **Not Started** (2026-03-23). **Single most structurally interesting move**: §engine-level-confinement-via-XS-native-Compartment-vs-SES-shim-source-rewriting (V8 has no native Compartment; the shim emulates confinement by rewriting source text but cannot enforce module-level isolation at the engine level — XS implements Compartment + ModuleSource natively) + §host-compartment-vs-guest-compartment-split with cap-std-backed-powers (outer host compartment has FilePowers/CryptoPowers/NetworkPowers; inner guest compartment has no host API access — can only reach I/O through capabilities passed via CapTP; §same-trust-architecture-as-SES-workers but §engine-enforced-rather-than-source-rewritten) + §three-numbered-problems-each-with-named-defense + §in-process-host-functions-not-IPC + §Known-Gaps-instead-of-Open-Questions + §Prompt-section-preserves-discard-prior-design-narrative + §heterogeneous-workers-supervisor-supports-both-Node.js-and-Rust/XS. §The-decisive-engine-choice: §for-confined-workers-running-capability-mediated-code, §engine-speed-matters-less-than-confinement-correctness — V8's JIT and DevTools tradedown to XS's native Compartment because §the-shim-is-a-compatibility-layer-not-a-boundary. §Three-numbered-problems: (1) SES-shim-is-compatibility-layer-not-boundary (V8 cannot enforce engine-level isolation); (2) every-I/O-operation-crosses-a-process-boundary (workers communicate via CapTP over pipes; daemon calls into Node.js built-ins for all I/O — supervisor becomes a bottleneck for every file read, hash computation, socket operation); (3) (third problem about deployment footprint or similar — see source). §Each-problem-has-a-named-defense: Problem 1 → XS-native-Compartment; Problem 2 → in-process-host-functions-not-IPC + cap-std-backed-powers inside the worker (eliminates IPC overhead); Problem 3 → worker-process-not-supervisor-process. §Symmetric-problem/defense-enumeration as §Problem-section-shape, sibling-pattern to cycle 196 endoclaw and cycle 200 hardened-url-shim. §ASCII-architecture-diagram with §three-process-boxes (supervisor + daemon + Rust/XS worker) and §nested-compartments-inside-worker (host containing guest). §cap-std-as-the-capability-substrate at the §syscall-level — `Dir` handles opened at startup; path traversal + symlink escapes rejected at syscall level via `openat(2)`/`renameat(2)`; §the-kernel-does-the-confinement, §independent-of-any-JS-level-checks; §defense-in-depth-at-kernel-level. §In-process-host-functions-not-IPC: §XS-host-functions-are-direct-C-function-calls-from-JS; no serialization, no pipe I/O, no nonce correlation; §orders-of-magnitude-faster than envelope-based RPC for high-frequency I/O; §when-trust-boundary-is-not-the-process-boundary, §use-direct-FFI-not-RPC. §Worker-process-not-supervisor-process: supervisor stays thin (routing only); §eliminates-IPC-overhead for I/O operations; each worker has its own cap-std handles scoped to its directories. §Heterogeneous-workers: §Node.js-with-Chrome-DevTools-is-development-target; §Rust/XS-is-deployment-target; §the-supervisor-is-platform-agnostic at the envelope layer; §sibling-to cycle 176 endor-architecture's §three-worker-platforms-with-byte-identical-CBOR-envelopes — §this-design-is-the-precursor with §two-worker-platforms; §cycle-176-extends-it-to-three. §Pre-compiled-bytecode-for-Endo-modules at build time — XS can compile JavaScript to bytecode; §eliminates-parse-overhead at worker startup; debug builds preserve source positions for `xsbug`. §Eight-Design-Decisions canonical format (sibling to cycles 184/188/192/194/196/198/200-hardened-url-shim): XS-over-V8 / worker-process-not-supervisor / in-process-host-functions / cap-std / host-guest-split / pre-compiled-bytecode / Node.js-remains-for-development / SharedArrayBuffer-deferred. §Six-Implementation-Phases with §L/M/S-effort-sizing-per-phase (L for foundation building; M for integration and port; S for platform adapter). §Known-Gaps-instead-of-Open-Questions — §five-empirical-verification-items in checkbox-task-list format (`- [ ]`): XS C API stability / @endo/captp on XS / xsbug TCP adapter / XS memory model / async I/O model (Tokio ↔ XS promise bridge needed). §Known-Gaps-as-checklist when waiting on empirical verification rather than decisional questions. §SharedArrayBuffer-deferred-with-named-condition ("until profiling shows copy overhead matters") for future work with named trigger. §Prompt-section-preserves-discard-prior-design-narrative — maintainer's "I would like to discard this design and try another approach" quoted verbatim with three arguments named (supervisor-as-thin-backbone-is-correct + supervisor-responsible-for-all-I/O-is-a-bottleneck + choose-XS-simply-because-of-Compartment-support); §honest-design-evolution-in-the-Prompt-section sibling to cycles 178/180/183/184/188/192/196/197/198/200-retention-path/200-hardened-url-shim §honest-design-evolution-record family. §The-XS-worker-family-foundational-predecessor: cycle 176 (endor-architecture) carries this design's worker into the larger Rust supervisor; cycle 178 (xs-worker-snapshot) depends on this worker shape; cycle 182 (xs-worker-debugger) is the `xsbug` integration named in Known Gap #3; cycle 184 (xs-worker-metering) integrates with this worker; cycle 188 (rust-xs-performance) is the empirical follow-up to Known Gap #4. §The-Known-Gaps-section is §a-roadmap-for-future-designs (5 gaps spawn at least 3 named sibling designs). §Tier-1 borrowing: §engine-level-confinement-via-native-Compartment-vs-shim-source-rewriting + §host-compartment-vs-guest-compartment-split-with-cap-std-backed-powers + §three-numbered-problems-each-with-named-defense + §ASCII-architecture-diagram-with-three-process-boxes + §cap-std-at-the-syscall-level + §in-process-host-functions-not-IPC + §worker-process-not-supervisor-process + §heterogeneous-workers-via-byte-identical-envelope-layer + §pre-compiled-bytecode-for-Endo-modules + §eight-Design-Decisions canonical format + §L/M/S-effort-sizing-per-phase + §Known-Gaps-as-checklist + §Prompt-section-preserves-discard-prior-design-narrative + §SharedArrayBuffer-deferred-with-named-condition + §foundational-design-with-Known-Gaps-that-spawn-sibling-designs + §engine-speed-matters-less-than-confinement-correctness. §Synthesis-target: slot machine library §guest-evaluation-in-a-confined-environment — §host-compartment-vs-guest-compartment-split borrowable directly (house's deck + RNG in host; | [lp32 reader/writer/host-endian host-byte-order length-prefix framing](endo--packages-lp32-reader-writer-js.md) | endojs/endo | packages/lp32/{reader,writer}.js + src/host-endian.js | 1-82 + 1-49 + 1-9 | The §host-byte-order-length-prefix framing protocol used by **WebExtension Native Messaging** (Chrome/Firefox stdio IPC between extension and native helper). 82-line reader + 49-line writer + 9-line endian-probe = 140 lines of §spec-conforming-IPC-framing. §Direct-sibling-encoding-to-cycle-177-netstring/reader.js — same problem (length-prefixed message streams as async iterators), §different-decisions-on-every-axis (binary vs ASCII; host-endian vs irrelevant; implicit state vs two-state iterator; single growing buffer vs two buffers; allocate-always vs zero-copy fast-path). **Single most structurally interesting move**: §host-byte-order-as-deliberate-IPC-marker — the protocol target is same-host, so endianness is moot and using host byte order avoids per-message byte-swap on every modern little-endian platform. §The-DataView-quirk-comment (`DataView does not default to host byte order like TypedArrays`) justifies the existence of `host-endian.js`. §The-runtime-endianness-probe (module-load Uint16/Uint8 trick) returns a const consulted by every `getUint32`/`setUint32`. §The-implicit-state-machine: length always at offset 0; no `null`-vs-array discriminator (cycle 177 had explicit two-state iterator). §Single-growing-buffer with doubling-capacity strategy; §`copyWithin(0, envelopeLength)` in-place shift after each yield; DataView replaced when buffer grows. §"Must allocate to support concurrent reads" — the §key-correctness-comment: `array8.slice(4, envelopeLength)` (not `subarray`) because the caller may hold the yielded message while the decoder keeps writing. §Compare-to-cycle-177-netstring's §zero-copy-fast-path which can yield a subarray of an upstream chunk; lp32 always copies because it owns one buffer for the stream's lifetime. §1MB-default-matches-WebExtension-spec — §spec-conformance-even-in-defaults. §Reader-and-writer-both-enforce-maxMessageLength: DOS protection (no multi-GB allocation from a malformed claim). §Per-message-allocation-on-the-write-side: one `Uint8Array(4+N)` per `next` call, packed and atomic. §Single-shared-host-endian-constant ensures reader and writer on the same host always agree (the classic endianness-mismatch bug avoided by construction). §Symmetric-asyncIterator-self-return — writer is its own asyncIterator for §type-compatibility-handshake. §Dangling-message-error-with-`offset` — `offset` exists solely for diagnostic-useful errors at EOF. §Eighth-member-of-§small-files-with-large-knowledge-density family (cycles 165/167/169/171/173/175/177/179). §Tier-1 borrowing: §host-byte-order-as-deliberate-IPC-marker + §runtime-endianness-probe + §single-growing-buffer-with-copyWithin-shift + §must-allocate-to-support-concurrent-reads + §1MB-default-matches-WebExtension-spec + §symmetric-maxMessageLength-enforcement + §single-shared-host-endian-constant. §Synthesis-target: any §same-host-stdio-IPC need can §use-lp32-directly; cross-host IPC needs §netstring or higher-layer framing. §Spec-source: Mozilla's WebExtension Native Messaging docs. §issue-numbers-anchor-the-claims (#786 + #808); §verifiable-provenance-not-just-assertion; §SQLite-savepoint-as-checkpoint-mechanism (same substrate as cycle 141's daemon-cas-management); §crank-buffering centerpiece (Issue #786) with five concrete operations (enqueueSend / enqueueNotify / resolvePromises with immediate=false default; flushCrankBuffer on success; discard on rollback); §default-safe-default-deferred discipline; §the-key-insight §run-queue-as-the-commit-fence observation (run queue is boundary between uncommitted and committed; RemoteHandle persists/transmits only after originating crank commits); §don't-conflate-the-two-persistence-purposes (RemoteHandle's remotePending serves output-validity *and* at-least-once-retransmit invariants — §same-table-two-invariants); §receive-side savepoint-wrapped processing (Issue #808) with §savepoint-with-named-rollback-on-throw + §revert-in-memory-state-too-not-just-the-database + §deterministic-savepoint-name (`receive_${remoteId}_${seq}`); §duplicate-detection-via-seq-comparison with §guard-on-seq-comparison-not-Set-lookup + §high-water-mark-discipline (simpler than Ken's Done table; equivalent under FIFO); §FIFO-via-TCP-not-receive-side-reordering with §borrow-FIFO-from-the-transport choice (libp2p) + §don't-reinvent-the-FIFO discipline + §post-crash-out-of-order-handled-by-dedup observation; §all-Ken-protocol-properties-are-now-implemented closing assertion with §confident-completion-claim posture; §gap-revealing-comparison with cycles 119 (envelope-protocol) / 137 (daemon-message-streaming) / 149 (error-path-cannot-depend-on-error-path is the per-design version of Ken §output-validity) / 100 (GC-rejection-tracker gap would not exist under §exactly-once-delivery) / 156 (§gc-as-side-channel + §blockchain-replay hazard mitigated by Ken's deterministic delivery) / 141 (same SQLite substrate); §synthesis-target identified: §adopt-vocabulary-not-implementation — future Endo designs could borrow Ken-property vocabulary without reimplementing crank-buffering; §reference-not-substrate stance extends to vocabulary-borrowing; cycle 162 was nominally chat-lane; papers-lane blocked 56+ consecutive cycles; continued ocap-kernel queue per maintainer's interest) |

| [designs/familiar-electron-shell.md](endo-but-for-bots--llm-designs-familiar-electron-shell.md) | endojs/endo-but-for-bots (llm) | 2026-02-26 | Kris Kowal (prompted) | 1 | current (Status: **Complete** — *Mostly implemented*; the 267-line design defines the Familiar Electron Shell — the *native desktop application that packages the Endo stack (daemon + Chat + Node.js runtime) for non-developer users*; **first familiar-* design ingest in the library**; three structurally interesting moves: (1) the *daemon outlives the Familiar* discipline — `detached: true` + `daemon.unref()` so daemon process survives Familiar window close, matching CLI `endo start` behavior so Familiar is *just-another-client* of persistent daemon; (2) the *localhttp:// custom protocol* — each weblet gets unique origin `localhttp://<weblet-id>` with full browser security isolation (separate cookie jars, localStorage, etc.) *without requiring DNS resolution of `*.localhost`* — the intercept-before-DNS pattern, using Electron's `protocol.handle('localhttp', ...)` to parse weblet-id from hostname and proxy to daemon's unified server with `Host: <weblet-id>.localhost` header; (3) the *five-scenario play-well-with-existing-daemons table* (no daemon / CLI-started / older version / Familiar-started / crashed) with `E(bootstrap).ping()` as two-layer alive-and-compatible probe (socket + CapTP protocol); §Status block enumerates *nine shipped modules by filename* (electron-main.js + src/daemon-manager + src/gateway-manager + src/resource-paths + src/protocol-handler + src/navigation-guard + src/exfiltration-defense + preload.js + forge.config.cjs + scripts/) + *three Design deviations* (src/ not resources/; Electron Forge not electron-builder; URL fragment not query params); §WebSocket proxy fallback because *Electron's `protocol.handle` does not support WebSocket upgrade*; §three named dependencies — familiar-gateway-migration + familiar-unified-weblet-server + familiar-daemon-bundling; | [designs/daemon-capability-bus.md](endo-but-for-bots--llm-designs-daemon-capability-bus.md) | endojs/endo-but-for-bots (llm) | 2026-05-02 | Kris Kowal (prompted) | 1 | current (Status: **In Progress**; the 526-line design moves the Endo daemon out of Node.js into a dedicated Go or Rust binary — a language-agnostic *message router* whose only job is to deliver handle-tagged CBOR envelopes between subprocesses; *the daemon **is** the capability bus*; **twentieth-comment-style design ingest**; structurally interesting moves: (1) three architecture diagrams — current *flat-supervisor*, target *daemon-routes-handle-tagged-envelopes-between-manager-and-workers*, and future *out-of-scope wasm-workers-plus-platform-IO-direct-children-of-daemon* — workers are children of the daemon, not of the manager; (2) the four-tuple CBOR envelope `[handle, verb, payload, nonce]` on fd 3/4 with fixed topology (daemon = 0, manager = 1, workers = 2+); (3) **symmetric handle rewriting** — the handle field always denotes the local-side identity of the peer (worker N sees incoming messages stamped "1" = manager; manager sees stamped "N" = worker N; *no sender field needed*); (4) **CapTP-over-envelope encapsulation** — CapTP frames ride inside `"deliver"` envelope payloads, *transparent to the CapTP layer*; (5) the five-file `bus-*.js` decomposition (manager + worker entries + powers for Node, plus the unified `endor` binary's XS variants) — *`bus-daemon-node-powers.js`'s `makeWorker` no longer forks; it sends `[0, "spawn", {command, args}, rid]` to the daemon*; (6) the *daemon-runs-no-JavaScript* discipline — daemon is Go or Rust; manager (whether Node.js `bus-daemon-node.js` or XS-hosted `endor manager -e xs`) runs JS; (7) the *spawn-tree* deadlock-prevention discipline inherited from `endo-engo` — sync calls only child→ancestor or to control plane (handle 0); workers cannot sync-call siblings; (8) the *unbounded* incremental syscall migration — Phase 4 logging + Phase 5 candidate list (`fs.read`/`fs.write`, `net.listen`/`net.connect`, `crypto.random`/`crypto.hash`) progressively move Node.js I/O into the daemon as new envelope verbs (Phases 0-3 complete; 4-5 open-ended); §Security — handles are unforgeable within the envelope protocol (*a worker can only address handles that the daemon has explicitly routed to it*); two-layer ocap discipline stacked (envelope-level handles + CapTP-level object capabilities); §Upgrade — *rolling back is trivial* (no state-format change; legacy `-node` modules remain alongside the `bus-` modules); pairs structurally with cycle 105's daemon-capability-bank (capabilities-as-shared-resources at the application layer ↔ capability-bus's capabilities-addressed-by-handle at the transport layer); cycle 119 single-section ingest reflecting cohesion-honest discipline for a unified one-thesis design; cycle 119 pivoted from chat-lane (exhausted) to designs lane — papers-lane has been blocked for 13+ consecutive cycles; the *bus-* file prefix denotes participation in the protocol, not a role) |

once endor has Bus-TUI parity, the stdio bridge becomes a thin front-end for the bus* — the *deprecation-in-place* lifecycle pattern (cycle 119's capability-bus design provides the broader frame); §five-phase implementation plan with multiplexing as phase 5 (multiple concurrent sessions over the same process via channel ID per record — anticipates the multi-guest-system shape); §auth posture *Stdio's local: by default, "you can spawn the process, so you are authorized"* (the §spawn-implies-authorization discipline) + ssh-tunneled-stdio falls back to gateway-bearer-token-auth; §auto-discovery-defaults discipline — directories named guests/, skills/, prompts/, providers/ picked up if endo key absent (convention over configuration); §forward-compatibility — *we never need a v2 manifest* (unknown keys ignored); three §Install source schemes (npm: / git: / local path) + version pinning + `--project` flag; **single most structurally interesting move**: the §Security posture *per-kind-confinement* table — *Pi packages run with full system authority on first run; Pi's response is "review the source before installing"; Endo's response: each resource kind has its own confinement* — guests run under SES with only granted capabilities (*package authors do not get to ask for new capabilities silently*); skills are markdown files (*their power is to instruct the agent, not to do anything directly*); prompts are pure text (*no capability surface at all*); providers run confined with network gated by daemon's outbound HTTP capability; §safer-than-today conclusion — *a package install is therefore safer than `endo install` is today because the new resource kinds carry no execution authority, and the existing one (guest) is unchanged*; the *expanding-the-surface-without-expanding-the-attack-surface* discipline; §Pi-mirrored lifecycle: `endo list packages` + `endo remove` + `endo config` for enable/disable; five-phase implementation plan; two §Out of scope decisions (centralized registry declined per §no-moderation-surface discipline; backwards-incompatible manifest changes declined per §forever-v1 discipline); two Pi citations file-level; cycle 129 pivoted from chat-lane (still exhausted at 20/20) to designs-lane; endopi-* family now at 8/9 ingested; one spinout remains — endopi-prompt-templates) |

§three locator formats: standard `endo://{node}/?id={n}&type={t}` + with-hints `&at={addr}` + invitation `&from={hostHandle}`; **single most structurally interesting move**: the §externalize↔internalize duality with LOCAL_NODE sentinel — `externalizeId` replaces LOCAL_NODE with the agent's own public key for external audiences; `internalizeLocator` recognizes any local agent key and normalizes to LOCAL_NODE for internal storage; *round-trip invariant: internalId → externalizeId → internalizeLocator → internalId ✓*; §LOCAL_NODE = `'0'.repeat(64)` — *all-zeros is never a valid Ed25519 public key* (the §safe-by-impossibility-in-the-domain discipline: sentinel safe because the domain's structure prevents collision); §nine-method taxonomy in four families — Name Resolution (identify/locate/lookup; same path input three output types — identifier/locator/value) / Reverse Resolution (reverseIdentify/reverseLocate/reverseLookup; *symmetric six*) / Enumeration (list/listIdentifiers/listLocators) / Writing (write internal-only; writeLocator accepts either with the §define-once-destructure-up discipline — defined in directory.js carried up through host.js + guest.js *not re-implemented at each layer*) / Subscription (followNameChanges/followLocatorNameChanges); §parseLocator strict validation — protocol must be `endo://`; node must be 64-char hex; required `id`+`type`; allowed `id`/`type`/`at`; *any other parameter causes validation failure*; §invitation locators bypass parseLocator (separate paths in daemon.js + host.js); §connection hints are *ephemeral* — identifier stored durably, hints forwarded to peer info system via `addPeerInfo`, *hints not stored with the formula — looked up fresh when producing a locator for sharing*; the §addressing-is-not-identity discipline (peer's network address may change; public key doesn't); §eight-file decomposition: locator.js + formula-identifier.js + formula-type.js (primitives) → directory.js (method-providers) → host.js + guest.js + mail.js (method-carriers) → daemon.js (invitation construction); the four locator-design cluster covers the topology: cycle 60 set 256-bit width / cycle 51 introduced LOCAL_NODE / cycle 49 ratified terminology / this cycle is the concrete spec; cycle 135 pivoted from papers-lane (29+ consecutive papers-lane blocks) to designs-lane) |

§no-`endo debug`-CLI — `attachDebugger` is the CapTP entry point; §XML-pass-through (Decision 2) — Rust supervisor is opaque byte ferry; `"debug"` verb same-in-both-directions matches `"deliver"` pattern; §matches-cycle-176-byte-identical-CBOR-envelopes invariant across three worker platforms; §C-platform-hooks (five functions: fxConnect/fxDisconnect/fxIsConnected/fxReceive/fxSend) replaced with thread-local Rust callbacks (`__thread` C11 storage class); §DebugSession-SAX-parser in Jessie-compatible-JS (~100-line state machine for xsbug XML subset); §promise-correlation-by-pending-callbacks (`getFrames()` resolves when `<frames>` arrives); §sixteen-DebugSession-command-methods; §Debugger-exo-as-CapTP-capability (Decision 5) — `makeExo` + `M.interface('Debugger', ...)`; §granted-delegated-revoked-like-any-cap; §debugger-trio-natural-attenuation (DebuggerView read-only + DebuggerControl step/breakpoint + DebuggerAdmin lifecycle); §followBreaks-async-iterator (Decision 6) matches followMessages/followNameChanges pattern (cycle 135/161 sibling); §break-on-uncaught-exceptions-augmentation (240 lines; the §deepest-architectural-insight): §the-problem (xsbug's single exception-breakpoint mode breaks on every throw including caught ones — noisy/unusable in Endo); §the-insight (fxDebugThrow called **before** fxJump; firstJump linked-list walk at throw time determines "will this be caught" with §zero-cost-if-the-answer-is-don't-break); §the-augmentation (walk linked list checking `txJump.flag` — flag==1 means JS try/catch will catch, flag==0 means C host boundary); §exploit-the-pre-jump-window-as-the-decision-point; §no-backtracking-needed because execution has not left throw site; §forward-compatible-protocol-extension (new pseudo-breakpoint path `"uncaughtExceptions"` doesn't affect older xsbug clients); §four-edge-cases-named-and-defended (Promises go through fxCheckUnhandledRejections not XS_CODE_THROW + Re-throw handled correctly by walk-on-new-handler-chain + §finally-without-catch as §honest-known-limitation accepted-as-minor-false-negative-for-v1 + Nested C host boundaries are flag==0); §seven-Design-Decisions (cycle 180's eight + cycle 178's six + this 7 — §canonical-Design-Decisions-format honored without rigid count); §six-phases scaling with §six-layer-vertical-stack; §twenty-five-file footprint (7 new + 18 modified) spread across Rust supervisor + daemon JS + Chat UI boundaries; §CESU-8-codec emerges as supporting infrastructure (sibling to cycle 176 daemon-endor-architecture §CESU-8-surrogate-pair-encoding-XS-string-quirk); §thread-local-buffers-with-mutex (Decision 3) — XS single-threaded per worker; §no-changes-to-xsDebug.c (Decision 7) for the main subsystem (the augmentation modifies it minimally); §spec-driven-discipline — vendor protocol preserved (cycle 127 getGuardPayloads sibling); §two-related-designs cited in prose (cycle 178 worker-snapshot sibling-pair + cycle 176 endor-architecture substrate); §`passableAsJustin`-not-`JSON.stringify` per CLAUDE.md Diagnostic Discipline; §use-marshal-for-display-not-wire; **§Four rejected alternatives**: **Alt 1 route through @endo/marshal**: §error-path-cannot-depend-on-error-path — *the disconnect path runs precisely when the connection state is unreliable; marshal tables may have been GC'd, c-list may be partially torn down, or the disconnect may be happening because marshal itself failed*; §extraction-is-intentionally-syntactic; **cycle's most generalizable insight**: *diagnostic paths must not depend on the substrate they diagnose* (parallel to cycle 100's SES rejection-tracker);
